Proverbs 1:1

The proverbs of Solomon son of David, who reigned in Israel,

  1. 1 Kings 2:12 historical identity

    Proverbs identifies Solomon as David's son and Israel's king; Kings records Solomon taking the throne of his father David, supplying the royal family setting of this attribution.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 2:12 Solomon sat on the throne of his father David when he was twelve years old, and his kingdom was firmly prepared.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:2

for knowing wisdom and discipline and understanding words of good sense,

  1. Proverbs 23:12 wisdom instruction parallel

    The opening purpose is to know wisdom and discipline and understand sensible words; the later proverb directs the heart to discipline and the ears to words of discernment.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 23:12 Give your heart to discipline, and prepare your ears for words of discernment.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:3

for receiving turns of speech, understanding true righteousness, and directing judgment rightly,

  1. Proverbs 2:9 literary purpose and outcome

    The book promises instruction in righteousness and right judgment; chapter 2 describes understanding righteousness and judgment as the outcome of receiving wisdom.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 2:9 Then you will understand righteousness and judgment, and you will make all good courses straight.

    Pinned source record

  2. 1 Kings 3:9-12 narrative illustration

    The stated aim of understanding righteousness and directing judgment has a concrete counterpart in Solomon's request to judge the people in righteousness and God's gift of a discerning, wise heart.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:9 Give Thy servant a heart to hear and to judge Thy people in righteousness, discerning between good and evil; for who can judge this weighty people of Thine?”

    1 Kings 3:10 It pleased the Lord that Solomon had asked this.

    1 Kings 3:11 The Lord said to him, “Because you have asked this of Me and have not asked for many days for yourself, or wealth, or the lives of your enemies, but have asked for understanding to hear judgment,

    1 Kings 3:12 behold, I have done according to your word. Behold, I have given you a discerning and wise heart, so that no one like you has been before you, and no one like you shall arise after you.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:4

so that he may give shrewdness to the innocent, perception and thought to a young child.

  1. Psalms 118:130 instructional parallel

    Proverbs aims to give shrewdness to the innocent and perception and thought to a young child; the psalm says the disclosure of God's words gives light and understanding to the simple. Both link instruction with perception or understanding while preserving the different descriptions of its recipients.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 118:130 The disclosure of Thy words will give light and understanding to the simple.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:5

For a wise person who hears these things will become wiser, and the discerning will acquire guidance,

  1. Proverbs 9:9 literary parallel

    Both sayings describe growth in an already wise hearer: the wise listener becomes wiser here, and the wise person given a starting point receives more wisdom in chapter 9.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 9:9 Give a wise person a starting point, and he will be wiser; inform a righteous person, and he will receive still more.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:6

and understand a comparison and an obscure saying, the utterances of the wise and riddles.

  1. Sirach 39:2-3 wisdom literature parallel

    Understanding comparisons, obscure sayings and riddles is paired with the sage's study of parables, hidden meanings of proverbs and their riddles in Sirach.

    Read matching passage

    Sirach 39:2 He will preserve the discourse of renowned men and enter into the turns of parables.

    Sirach 39:3 He will seek out the hidden meanings of proverbs and dwell among the riddles of parables.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:7

The fear of God is the beginning of wisdom, and good understanding belongs to all who practice it. Reverence toward God is the beginning of perception, but the ungodly will despise wisdom and discipline.

  1. Psalms 110:10 verbal parallel

    Both passages call fear of the Lord the beginning of wisdom and associate good understanding with practicing it; the shared formulation links reverence with enacted understanding.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 110:10 The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord; good understanding belongs to all who practice it. His praise remains for an age of an age.

    Pinned source record

  2. Proverbs 9:10 literary repetition

    The book later repeats the principle that fear of the Lord begins wisdom, connecting the opening statement with the counsel of holy ones in chapter 9.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 9:10 The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord, and the counsel of holy ones is understanding.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:8

Listen, son, to your father’s discipline, and do not reject your mother’s ordinances,

  1. Proverbs 6:20 literary repetition

    The paired commands to heed a father's instruction and not reject a mother's ordinances recur in chapter 6, preserving the distinctive instruction from both parents.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 6:20 Son, guard your father’s laws, and do not reject your mother’s ordinances.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:9

for you will receive a crown of graces for your head and a golden collar around your neck.

  1. Proverbs 4:9 adornment imagery

    Both passages picture received wisdom as a crown of graces placed on the learner's head; the promised collar here and crown of delight there expand the image of honored instruction.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 4:9 so that she may give your head a crown of graces and shield you with a crown of delight.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:10

Son, do not let ungodly men lead you astray; do not agree if they invite you, saying,

  1. Proverbs 4:14 ethical parallel

    The warning not to consent when ungodly men invite the son is reinforced by the later command not to enter ungodly ways or imitate lawless conduct.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 4:14 Do not enter the ways of the ungodly or imitate the ways of the lawless.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:11

Come with us; share in bloodshed. Let us hide a righteous man in the earth unjustly;

  1. Wisdom of Solomon 2:12-20 plot against the righteous

    Both speeches put a collective plan against a righteous person into the plotters' mouths: Proverbs proposes unjust bloodshed, while Wisdom develops an ambush ending in torture and a shameful death sentence.

    Read matching passage

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:12 Let us lie in wait for the righteous person, because he is troublesome to us and opposes our works; he reproaches us for sins against law and charges us with sins against our upbringing.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:13 He professes to have knowledge of God and calls himself a child of the Lord.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:14 He has become a reproach to our thoughts; even the sight of him weighs upon us,

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:15 because his life is unlike that of others, and his paths are different.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:16 He has reckoned us counterfeit, and keeps away from our ways as from impurities; he calls the final lot of the righteous blessed, and boasts that God is his father.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:17 Let us see whether his words are true, and test what will happen at his departure.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:18 For if the righteous person is a son of God, He will help him and rescue him from the hand of his opponents.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:19 Let us examine him with insult and torture, so that we may know his gentleness and test his endurance of wrong.

    Wisdom of Solomon 2:20 Let us condemn him to a shameful death, for, according to his words, he will be visited with care.”

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:12

let us swallow him alive like Hades and remove his memory from the earth.

  1. Numbers 16:30-33 death imagery

    The robbers boast of swallowing someone alive like Hades; Numbers describes the earth swallowing the rebels and their descent alive to Hades. The shared image connects a murderous boast with the account of divine judgment.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 16:30 But if the Lord displays a portent, and the earth opens its mouth and swallows them, their households, their tents, and everything that belongs to them, and they go down alive to Hades, then you shall know that these men have provoked the Lord.

    Numbers 16:31 As he finished speaking all these words, the ground beneath them split apart.

    Numbers 16:32 And the earth opened and swallowed them, their households, all the people who were with Korah, and their livestock.

    Numbers 16:33 And they and all that belonged to them went down alive to Hades; the earth covered them, and they perished from the midst of the congregation.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:13

Let us seize his costly property and fill our houses with spoils.

  1. Micah 2:2 predatory acquisition

    The plan to seize costly property and fill houses with spoils parallels Micah's condemnation of coveting fields and plundering a man, his house and his inheritance.

    Read matching passage

    Micah 2:2 They coveted fields and plundered orphans, oppressed houses and plundered a man and his house, a man and his inheritance.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:14

Cast your lot among us; let us all obtain a common purse, and let there be one moneybag for us.

  1. Psalms 49:18 criminal association

    The invitation to cast one's lot with plunderers and share their purse is paired with the psalm's condemnation of running with a thief and placing one's portion among wrongdoers.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 49:18 If you saw a thief, you would run along with him, and you would place your portion with adulterers.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:15

Do not walk in the way with them; turn your foot away from their paths,

  1. Psalms 1:1 way and association

    Turning one's foot away from the criminals' paths corresponds to the blessed person's refusal to walk in ungodly counsel or stand in the way of sinners.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 1:1 Blessed is the man who did not walk in the counsel of the ungodly, and did not stand in the way of sinners, and did not sit in the seat of pestilent men;

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:16

for their feet run toward evil, and they are quick to shed blood.

  1. Isaiah 59:7 verbal parallel

    Both passages describe feet running toward evil and being quick to shed blood, making the same bodily image a description of readiness for violence.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 59:7 Their feet run to wickedness, quick to shed blood. Their thoughts are the thoughts of fools; destruction and misery lie in their ways.

    Pinned source record

  2. Romans 3:15 verbal parallel

    Romans includes feet swift to pour out blood in its scriptural description of sinfulness, closely matching Proverbs' feet quick to shed blood.

    Read matching passage

    Romans 3:15 Their feet are swift to pour out blood;

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:18

For those who share in murder store up evils for themselves, and the overthrow of lawless men is evil.

  1. Psalms 7:16-17 reversal of wrongdoing

    The murderers store up evils for themselves; the psalm depicts the wrongdoer falling into his own pit and his injustice returning upon his head. Both locate the harm's return upon its planner.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 7:16 He dug a pit and hollowed it out, and he will fall into the hole he made.

    Psalms 7:17 His trouble will return upon his head, and his injustice will descend upon his crown.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:19

These are the ways of all who carry out lawless deeds, for through ungodliness they take away their own life.

  1. Proverbs 15:27 self-destructive gain

    The warning that lawless acquisition costs its practitioners their own life is paired with the later saying that taking bribes destroys the taker.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 15:27 One who takes bribes destroys himself, but one who hates taking gifts is saved.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:20

Wisdom is celebrated at the exits, and in the squares she speaks openly.

  1. Proverbs 8:2-4 personified wisdom's public call

    Wisdom speaks openly in the squares here; chapter 8 places her at paths and rulers' gates and has her send her voice to humankind. Both present wisdom as publicly accessible instruction.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 8:2 For she is on the high summits, and stands between the paths.

    Proverbs 8:3 For she sits beside the gates of rulers, and is praised at the entrances:

    Proverbs 8:4 “You, O human beings, I summon, and I send forth my voice to the children of humankind.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:21

At the tops of walls she is proclaimed; at rulers’ gates she sits nearby, and at city gates she speaks boldly:

  1. Proverbs 8:3 shared literary setting

    Wisdom's position at rulers' and city gates recurs in chapter 8, where she sits beside rulers' gates and is praised at the entrances.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 8:3 For she sits beside the gates of rulers, and is praised at the entrances:

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:22

As long as the innocent hold to righteousness, they will not be ashamed. But the senseless, desiring insolence, became ungodly and hated perception,

  1. Proverbs 1:29 same-chapter literary parallel

    The speech's accusation that the senseless hated perception is resumed by the later explanation that they hated wisdom and refused fear of the Lord.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 1:29 for they hated wisdom and did not choose the fear of the Lord,

Proverbs 1:23

and became liable to reproofs. Behold, I will send forth to you an utterance of my breath, and I will teach you my word.

  1. Proverbs 8:6-9 personified wisdom's teaching

    Wisdom promises to send forth an utterance and teach her word; her later speech invites listeners to hear the upright, truthful and righteous words proceeding from her lips.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 8:6 Listen to me, for I will speak noble things, and bring forth upright things from my lips.

    Proverbs 8:7 For my throat will meditate on truth, and lying lips are detestable before me.

    Proverbs 8:8 All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked or entangled in them.

    Proverbs 8:9 They are all plain to those who understand, and upright to those who find knowledge.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:24

Since I kept calling and you did not obey, and kept extending words and you paid no attention,

  1. Isaiah 65:12 rejected calls for obedience

    Wisdom describes repeatedly calling without obedience; Isaiah's judgment likewise answers the failure to obey when the Lord called and the disregard shown when He spoke.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 65:12 I will hand you over to the sword; all of you will fall in slaughter, because I called you and you did not obey, I spoke and you disregarded Me. You did evil before Me and chose what I did not want.”

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:25

but you were making my counsels ineffective and disobeyed my reproofs,

  1. Psalms 49:17 rejected instruction

    Ignoring wisdom's counsel and reproof corresponds to the sinner's hatred of instruction and casting God's words behind him in the psalm's rebuke.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 49:17 But you hated instruction and cast My words behind you.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:26

therefore I too will laugh at your ruin and exult when destruction comes to you,

  1. Psalms 2:4-5 judgment imagery

    Wisdom's laughter at the rejecters' ruin parallels the heavenly Lord's laughter and scorn before speaking in anger to rebellious rulers. Both use derision to announce judgment upon resistance.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 2:4 The One dwelling in heaven will laugh at them, and the Lord will sneer at them.

    Psalms 2:5 Then He will speak to them in His anger and trouble them in His fury:

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:27

and when sudden tumult reaches you and overthrow arrives like a storm, when affliction and siege come upon you, or when destruction comes to you.

  1. Proverbs 10:25 storm and ruin

    The sudden overthrow arriving like a storm is paired with the later proverb in which the passing storm removes the impious while the righteous is saved.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 10:25 When a storm passes by, the impious disappears, but the righteous turns aside and is saved for ever.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:28

For when you call upon me, I will not listen to you; evil people will seek me and will not find me,

  1. Micah 3:4 unanswered cry after wrongdoing

    Both passages announce that wrongdoers will cry for help and receive no hearing; Micah explicitly links the refusal to their wicked treatment of others.

    Read matching passage

    Micah 3:4 so they will cry to the Lord, but He will not listen to them. He will turn His face away from them at that time, because they acted wickedly in their practices against them.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:29

for they hated wisdom and did not choose the fear of the Lord,

  1. Job 28:28 wisdom and reverence

    The rejecters refuse wisdom and fear of the Lord; Job defines reverence for God as wisdom, clarifying the connection between the two things they reject.

    Read matching passage

    Job 28:28 And He said to a human, ‘Behold, reverence for God is wisdom, and abstaining from evils is knowledge.’”

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:30

nor did they want to attend to my counsels, but they were sneering at my reproofs.

  1. Proverbs 1:25 same-chapter literary refrain

    The refusal to attend to counsel and the sneering at reproof repeat the earlier accusation of making counsel ineffective and disobeying reproof within Wisdom's speech.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 1:25 but you were making my counsels ineffective and disobeyed my reproofs,

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:31

Therefore they will eat the fruits of their own way and be filled with their own ungodliness.

  1. Galatians 6:7-8 moral harvest imagery

    Eating the fruit of one's own way is paired with reaping what one sows; Galatians makes the adverse harvest explicit as corruption from sowing into the flesh.

    Read matching passage

    Galatians 6:7 Do not be deceived; God is not mocked. For whatever a person sows, that he will also reap.

    Galatians 6:8 For the one who sows into his own flesh will reap corruption from the flesh, but the one who sows into the Spirit will reap eternal life from the Spirit.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:32

For because they wronged the innocent, they will be murdered, and examination will destroy the ungodly.

  1. Psalms 36:14-15 violence returning upon attackers

    Proverbs says those who wronged the innocent will themselves be murdered; the psalm depicts attackers drawing weapons against the upright and then calls for their own sword to enter their heart.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 36:14 The sinners drew a sword and stretched their bow, to cast down the poor and needy, to slaughter the upright in heart.

    Psalms 36:15 May their sword enter their own heart, and may their bows be shattered.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 1:33

But whoever listens to me will dwell in hope and rest fearlessly from every evil.

  1. Proverbs 3:24-26 wisdom and freedom from fear

    Listening to Wisdom promises fearless rest here; chapter 3 develops secure sitting and sleep, freedom from sudden fright and the Lord's support along one's ways.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 3:24 For if you sit, you will be fearless, and if you sleep, you will sleep pleasantly.

    Proverbs 3:25 You will not fear an approaching fright or the oncoming assaults of the ungodly,

    Proverbs 3:26 for the Lord will be upon all your ways and support your foot so that you are not shaken.

Proverbs 2:6

For the Lord gives wisdom, and from His presence come knowledge and understanding.

  1. James 1:5 God as giver of wisdom

    Both passages expressly locate wisdom's gift in God: the Lord gives wisdom here, and James promises God's generous giving to the person who asks for it.

    Read matching passage

    James 1:5 But if any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask God, who gives to all generously and without reproach, and it will be given to him.

    Pinned source record

  2. 1 Kings 3:11-12 narrative illustration

    The statement that the Lord gives wisdom is illustrated by His response to Solomon's request for understanding: He gives Solomon a discerning and wise heart.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:11 The Lord said to him, “Because you have asked this of Me and have not asked for many days for yourself, or wealth, or the lives of your enemies, but have asked for understanding to hear judgment,

    1 Kings 3:12 behold, I have done according to your word. Behold, I have given you a discerning and wise heart, so that no one like you has been before you, and no one like you shall arise after you.

Proverbs 2:7

He stores up salvation for those acting rightly; He will shield their journey,

  1. Psalms 36:23-24 the Lord protecting the journey

    The Lord will shield the journey of those acting rightly; the psalm says the Lord directs a person's steps and supports his hand so that when he falls he will not be dashed down. Both connect divine care with the person's course and protection from harm.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 36:23 A person’s steps are directed by the Lord, and He will desire his way.

    Psalms 36:24 When he falls, he will not be dashed down, for the Lord supports his hand.

    Pinned source record

    Psalms 120:8 The Lord will guard your coming in and your going out, from now and for ever.

Proverbs 2:14

who delight in evils and rejoice in evil distortion,

  1. Proverbs 10:23 pleasure in wrongdoing

    Delight and rejoicing in evil correspond to the fool doing evil things in laughter in chapter 10. Both portray pleasure in wrongdoing; the later proverb explicitly identifies its subject as a fool.

    Read matching passage

Proverbs 2:15

whose paths are crooked and whose tracks are bent,

    Job 28:17 Gold and glass will not equal it, nor will golden vessels be its exchange.

  1. Psalms 103:8 creation imagery

    Wisdom precedes the mountains and hills here, while the psalm depicts mountains rising to the place God founded for them.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 103:8 Mountains rise and plains descend to the place Thou didst found for them.

Proverbs 8:26

    The Lord's making lands and inhabited extremities corresponds to the appearance and naming of the dry land as earth.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 1:9 And God said, “Let the water beneath heaven be gathered into one place, and let the dry land appear.” And it was so. And the water beneath heaven was gathered into its gathering places, and the dry land appeared.

    Genesis 1:10 And God called the dry land Earth, and the gathered waters He called Seas. And God saw that it was good.

Proverbs 8:31

when He rejoiced after completing the inhabited world, and delighted in the children of humankind.

  1. Genesis 1:26-31 creation comparison

    God's delight in the completed inhabited world and humankind corresponds to the creation of humans and His judgment that all He made was very good.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 1:26 And God said, “Let Us make man in Our image and likeness; and let them rule over the fish of the sea, the birds of heaven, the cattle, all the earth, and every creeping thing that creeps upon the earth.”

    Genesis 1:27 And God made man; in the image of God He made him; male and female He made them.

    Genesis 1:28 And God blessed them, saying, “Grow and multiply, and fill the earth and subdue it. Rule over the fish of the sea, the birds of heaven, all the cattle, all the earth, and every creeping thing that creeps upon the earth.”

    Genesis 1:29 And God said, “Behold, I have given you every plant sowing seed upon all the earth, and every tree that bears fruit containing seed. These shall be yours for food.

    Genesis 1:30 And to every wild beast of the earth, every bird of heaven, and every creeping thing upon the earth that has life within it, I have given every green plant for food.” And it was so.

    Genesis 1:31 And God saw all that He had made, and behold, it was very good. And there was evening, and there was morning: a sixth day.

Proverbs 21:31

A horse is prepared for a day of war, but help is from the Lord.

  1. Psalms 19:8-9 military resources and divine help

    The prepared warhorse is distinguished from help that comes from the Lord in Proverbs; the psalm contrasts reliance on horses and chariots with the Lord's name, followed by falling or being raised upright.

    Read matching passage

    Psalms 19:8 These rely on chariots, and those on horses, but we will be magnified in the name of the Lord our God.

    Psalms 19:9 They were fettered and fell, but we rose and were set upright.

Proverbs 22:1

A good name is more desirable than much wealth, and good favor is above silver and gold.

  1. Ecclesiastes 7:1 value of a name

    Proverbs prefers a good name to much wealth and favor to precious metals; Ecclesiastes similarly ranks a name above good oil.

    Read matching passage

    Ecclesiastes 7:1 A name is better than good oil, and the day of death than the day of one’s birth.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 22:2

Rich and poor met one another, and the Lord made both.

  1. Job 31:13-15 shared human origin across status

    The proverb says God made both rich and poor; Job appeals to shared formation in the womb when considering his servants' legal case. Both set differences of status beside common human origin.

    Read matching passage

    Job 31:13 If I also despised the case of my manservant or maidservant when they were in judgment against me,

    Job 31:14 what will I do if the Lord makes an examination of me? If He also makes an inspection, what answer will I make?

    Job 31:15 Were they not also formed in the womb just as I was? We were formed in the same belly.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 22:3

A resourceful person, seeing an evil person severely punished, is himself instructed, but fools passed by and were penalized.

  1. Proverbs 19:25 instruction through observed punishment

    The resourceful observer instructed by an evil person's punishment corresponds to the fool made more resourceful by watching a pestilent person whipped in 19:25.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 19:25 When a pestilent person is whipped, a fool becomes more resourceful; but if you reprove an intelligent man, he will understand discernment.

Proverbs 22:5

Thistles and traps are in crooked ways, but one who guards his own life will keep away from them.

  1. Proverbs 16:17 guarding one's way and life

    Avoiding the thistles and traps of crooked ways preserves life in 22:5; 16:17 similarly connects turning from evil and guarding one's ways with keeping one's life.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 16:17 Paths of life turn aside from evils, and ways of righteousness are length of life. One who receives discipline will be amid good things, and one who keeps rebukes will be made wise. One who guards his own ways keeps his own life, and one who loves his life will spare his mouth.

Proverbs 22:8

One who sows worthless things will reap evils, and he will bring the blow of his works to completion.

  1. Galatians 6:7-8 sowing and reaping image

    Sowing worthless things and reaping evils corresponds to Paul's principle that a person reaps what he sows, illustrated by corruption reaped from sowing to the flesh.

    Read matching passage

    Galatians 6:7 Do not be deceived; God is not mocked. For whatever a person sows, that he will also reap.

    Galatians 6:8 For the one who sows into his own flesh will reap corruption from the flesh, but the one who sows into the Spirit will reap eternal life from the Spirit.

  1. 2 Corinthians 9:7 verbal parallel in giving

    Read matching passage

  1. Proverbs 15:27 danger of gifts

    Proverbs 22:9a says the giver takes away the life of those possessing the gifts; 15:27 similarly connects taking bribes with the recipient's destruction, although 22:9a also describes honor for the giver.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 15:27 One who takes bribes destroys himself, but one who hates taking gifts is saved.

    Proverbs 6:3 Son, do what I command you and save yourself—for through your friend you have come into evil men’s hands. Go without growing slack, and press the friend for whom you acted as guarantor.

  1. Sirach 31:12-16 conduct at a great table

    Thoughtfully considering the food at rulers' tables corresponds to Sirach's instruction to consider matters at a great table and eat what is set before one without greedy grasping.

    Read matching passage

    Sirach 31:12 Have you sat at a great table? Do not open your throat over it or say, “How much there is upon it!”

    Sirach 31:13 Remember that an evil eye is bad. What has been created worse than an eye? Therefore it weeps for every reason.

    Sirach 31:14 Do not stretch your hand toward whatever it looks at, and do not crowd together with it in the dish.

    Sirach 31:15 Understand your neighbor’s concerns from yourself, and consider every matter.

    Sirach 31:16 Eat what is set before you like a human being, and do not chew greedily, lest you be hated.

Proverbs 23:32

But at the end he lies stretched out as though struck by a snake, and as though by a horned viper its poison spreads through him.

  1. Sirach 31:25-30 bodily harm of drunkenness

    In the continuing portrait of the drinker, Proverbs pictures collapse and spreading poison like a snakebite; Sirach likewise warns that wine destroys many and that drunkenness diminishes strength and adds wounds.

    Read matching passage

    Sirach 31:25 Do not show your manliness with wine, for wine has destroyed many.

    Sirach 31:26 A furnace tests hardened metal in quenching; so wine tests hearts in the quarrel of the proud.

    Sirach 31:27 Wine is like life for human beings if you drink it in its measure. What is life for someone lacking wine? It too was created for human gladness.

    Sirach 31:28 Wine drunk at the proper time in sufficient measure is rejoicing of heart and gladness of soul.

    Sirach 31:29 Wine drunk in quantity amid provocation and conflict is bitterness of soul.

    Sirach 31:30 Drunkenness multiplies a fool’s anger to his stumbling, diminishing strength and adding wounds.

Proverbs 23:34

    Proverbs compares the drinker's condition to a helmsman in heavy seas; the psalm conversely compares storm-tossed sailors' shaking and loss of wisdom to a drunken man.

    Psalms 106:27 They were troubled and shaken like a drunken man, and all their wisdom was swallowed up.

Proverbs 31:17

Having girded her waist strongly, she braced her arms for work.

  1. Proverbs 13:4 shared diligent-hands imagery

    The woman braces her arms for work; 13:4 similarly contrasts idle desire with the courageous person's hands actively engaged in care.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 13:4 Every idle person is amid desires, but the hands of the courageous are engaged in care.

Proverbs 31:18

She tasted that working is good, and her lamp is not extinguished throughout the night.

  1. Proverbs 14:23 thematic parallel

    The woman recognizes work's benefit and continues through the night; 14:23 likewise associates attentive labor with abundance and pleasure-seeking inactivity with want.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 14:23 In everyone who takes care there is abundance, but the pleasure-loving and unfeeling person will be in want.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:19

She stretches her forearms toward profitable things, and braces her hands on a spindle.

  1. Acts 9:39 narrative parallel

    The hands working a spindle belong to the same practical sphere as Dorcas' garment-making, where skilled handwork becomes useful provision for others.

    Read matching passage

    Acts 9:39 Peter rose and went with them. When he arrived, they brought him into the upper room. All the widows stood beside him weeping and showing the tunics and garments Dorcas had made while she was with them.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:20

She opened her hands to a needy person and stretched out her wrist to a poor person.

  1. Proverbs 19:17 ethical parallel

    Her hands are opened to the needy and poor; 19:17 explicitly commends mercy toward the poor as a gift whose repayment the Lord undertakes.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 19:17 One who has mercy on the poor lends to God, and He will repay him according to his gift.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:21

Her husband is not anxious about those at home when he stays away somewhere, for all those with her are clothed.

  1. 1 Timothy 5:8 household-provision parallel

    Her household is adequately clothed, relieving her husband's anxiety; First Timothy similarly treats provision for one's own household as a serious obligation.

    Read matching passage

    1 Timothy 5:8 But if someone does not provide for his own, and especially his household, he has denied the faith and is worse than an unbeliever.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:23

Her husband becomes distinguished in the gates when he sits in council with the elder inhabitants of the land.

  1. Proverbs 12:4 thematic parallel

    The husband's distinction among elders forms part of the portrait of the capable wife; 12:4 likewise calls a courageous woman her husband's crown.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 12:4 A courageous woman is a crown to her husband; but as a worm in wood, so a woman who does evil destroys a man.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:25

She opened her mouth attentively and lawfully and prescribed order for her tongue.

  1. Proverbs 13:3 thematic parallel

    The native verse describes attentive, lawful speech and a tongue kept in order; 13:3 likewise connects guarding the mouth with preservation of life.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 13:3 One who guards his own mouth keeps his own life, but one rash with his lips will terrify himself.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:27

The occupations of her houses are secure, and she did not eat idle food.

  1. Proverbs 12:11 thematic parallel

    Her secure household occupations and refusal of idle food correspond to 12:11's contrast between working one's land for bread and pursuing empty things.

    Read matching passage

    Proverbs 12:11 One who works his own land will be filled with loaves, but those who pursue empty things lack understanding.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:28

She opens her mouth wisely and according to law, and her mercy raised up her children, and they became wealthy, and her husband praised her:

  1. 1 Timothy 5:10 thematic parallel

    The woman's mercy in raising her children belongs with First Timothy's commendation of child-rearing, assistance to the afflicted, and a life attested by good works.

    Read matching passage

    1 Timothy 5:10 attested for good works: if she brought up children, if she received strangers, if she washed saints’ feet, if she assisted the afflicted, if she followed every good work.

    Pinned source record

Proverbs 31:30

A woman’s pleasing qualities are false and beauty is vain; for an understanding woman is blessed, and let her praise the fear of the Lord.

  1. 1 Peter 3:3-5 ethical contrast

    Proverbs ranks understanding and fear of the Lord above pleasing appearance; Peter similarly values the inward character of women who hope in God above outward adornment.

    Read matching passage

    1 Peter 3:3 Let your adornment not be the outward kind—braiding hair, putting on gold ornaments, or dressing in garments—

    1 Peter 3:4 but the hidden person of the heart, in the imperishable beauty of a gentle and quiet spirit, which is very precious before God.

    1 Peter 3:5 For this is how the holy women of former times, who hoped in God, also adorned themselves, submitting themselves to their own husbands,

    Pinned source record
